#11157 1 2006-09-10T11:55:17Z Clifty
JUDGING HAS FINISHED!!!

1st - Reign of Fire - By Clifty
2nd - Micro Cuts - By TheAdster
3rd - kakutouka - By scythe33.


This could be a contest that has never been done, despite the fact it is very simple.

RULES
  • You must submit your maps to NUMA, and post a link here.
  • All maps must be KRADDAs. (Keep the Rocket Alive, Don't Do Anything)
  • You can only submit 2 maps in the contest.
  • Judges can submit maps, but cannot vote for their own map.
JUDGING

The judging is very simple. Basically each of the judges picks any number of maps that they like, and they are all awarded one point. The map with the most points at the end is the winner. If there is a tie, all the judges revote for the tied maps. A judge cannot vote for their own map.
If you want to be a judge, post here.

JUDGES
  • Clifty
  • Psycho_CO
  • mc_george
  • Bluenin
ENTRIES
  • The Chaser's War By Clifty
  • The rockets' revenge By mikey210
  • This Monkey Wants a Word With You By Mongy
  • Shiri By Patsy7z7
  • Reign of Fire By Clifty
  • What Happens When You Leave a Rocket Alone In a Confined Space for 2 Days By earth159
  • kakutouka By scythe33
  • The Heart of the Machine 2: The Engine Room By lord_day
  • Duty Now For the Future By Mongy
  • Micro Cuts By TheAdster
If you need to ask me any questions, you know what to do.

#229146 14 2006-09-16T01:20:15Z Clifty
OK...
  • Launch pads: Do I really have to explain it?
  • Trap Doors: Turn on 1/4 snap, place the trap door key 3/4 of a space away from the door.
  • Bounce block: Put any number of bounce blocks down in the same place (normally about 1-15.) and then put a bit of gold on the corners.
  • Thwump: Use thwumps to push you around. If you can half squish the ninja between two thwumps, then he will go flying away.
  • Normal Doors: Put down 6 pieces of gold on a normal door. Sometimes you will die, sometimes you will bounce off, sometimes you will be pushed fowards. It all depends on the speed.
  • Glitch: Too hard to explain, because it contains code editing.
Did that help?

#240936 160 2006-10-14T09:15:17Z Clifty
(scythe33's Easy Way)
81-4, Sky=Limit.

OK, my first piece of advice would be to make the biggest jump that you can from the start. Just do some quick wall jumping, and you can skip more than half of it.

I think that you should probably stay still until the rockets have both exploded, then take the rest of the level slowly. If you jump into the dips in the map, and don't let the rockets follow, you shouldn't have much trouble. Also, be careful not to be hit by the second last thwump.

Then just repeat it on the way back.

[/easy way]

I always thought that 80-4 was harder, but its not me.



(scythe33's Easy Way)
65-4, Son of Pit of despair.

I'm going to assume you have trouble getting out of the pit, not the part at the top. If you have trouble at the top, my only advice is to take it very slowly.

I can't really describe how to get out, i'll just give a few tips:
  • When you walljump up a thwump, one way platform or door, you have to be pressing the key towards the object, or it will not work.
  • Try and walljump slowly. If you go to fast, you will go above the thwump, and probably hit a mine.
  • Obviously, if you go too slow, you will fall back down, or be electrocuted.
I can't think of anything else.

[/easy way]

#264813 5 2006-12-05T07:27:51Z Clifty
Some advice...
  • Don't use big lines of launchpads like that. I hate that.
  • Try to avoid gold delay. It isn't too bad, but you are better off without it.
  • Don't place pointless items (like all those unused enemies). They only create lag, and don't help the DDA at all.
  • Try to use varied propulsion. I liked the thwump propulsion, but try using trap doors and bounce block propulsion.
But, it was okay for your first.

#13043 1 2006-12-21T11:54:08Z Clifty
Types

.....Right, the way I see it, there are four different types of DDAs: Multi-enemy, KRADDA, concept, and n00b. I'm going to take time and try and describe all of them, and how I make them.


N00b

*shudders*

.....OK, a n00b DDA would be something like this. They have lines of launchpads, random Z snapping, and enemies which don't do anything. DO NOT MAKE THEM! THEY ARE TERRIBLE! If you want to make a DDA, at least put some effort into it.


Multi-Enemy

.....A standard DDA. A ninja, a bunch of enemies, and some propulsion. Not too hard to make once you get the idea. A good example would be this. Of course, the thing that makes M.E. DDAs great is close calls, so make sure you get plenty of those.
.....The way I make them is actually pretty simple. First you place down your enemies. Try to use as many as you can, but no more than 30 in total, or it will lag. Next, make a rule based on how you want the DDA to turn out. For example, normally I would say something like, "I'm not going to use any launchpads in this DDA," or, "I will make this go for at least 2500 frames," or both. Follow your rule no matter what. While you are making it, if you run into an enemy, don't block that enemy straight away! Try and propel the ninja away to another enemy. If you can't do that after several attempts, then block it off. Once you have no more available space, end it.


KRADDA

.....In case you don't already know, a KRADDA is a DDA where a rocket follows you for the entire level, like this. The key to making good KRADDAs is not only creating close calls for the ninja, but also close calls for the rocket, as it passes through gaps in walls. This is hard, but there is a trick to it. This is why KRADDAs with wide open spaces, are rarely good.
.....When you start making a KRADDA, I find it easier to start with an empty map, not a full one. I then need to pick a spot to add the ninja and the rocket, usually near eachother. I then just make it like a M.E. DDA (make a rule etc.) except I also try my best to get the rocket to go off on a different course to the ninja. When that happens, I place tiles around the area to make the rocket go in an extremely small area, hopefully between two tiles. (This is quite hard to explain, just watch the example above, and you should see what I mean.)
.....Once you have had some decent close calls for the rocket, and you have filled up as much of the screen as possible, you can place the exit.


Concept

.....OK, this is a pretty wide category. This is the category for new concepts for DDAs. An example would be this. It is basically when a DDA does something which has never (or rarely) been done before.
.....Of course, there isn't a trick to making these. You just need to come up with an imaginative concept. Don't try to make these, you can't really. You'll just stumble across a trick or a glitch some day, and then you can use it in a DDA.


NOTE: Some DDAs are mixtures of these four types. For example, this would be a cross between concept and KRADDA.

Propulsion

.....There are many different types of propelling the ninja while making DDAs. Launchpads aren't the only way around you know...


Launchpads

.....Doesn't even need explaining. Just point it where you want the ninja to go, and you're done.


Trap-doors

.....Right, there are two different types of trap door propulsions really, the type that speeds you up, and the one that reverses the direction you are already going in. For the first type, turn on C snap, and place the key 3/4 of a tile away from the door, in the direction you want the ninja to go in. For the other type, place the key half a space away from the door, on the opposite side of the door. (also hard to explain, you can see both types here). This also works by placing a normal door, and then putting a locked door on top of it, placing the key in similar spots.


Bounce blocks

.....This is a very simple propulsion, simply place down any number of bounce blocks in the same place (the more, the stronger) and put some gold on top (5 or more pieces.) This also works with thwumps instead of bounce blocks.


Normal doors

.....This is also simple, place a normal door down, and put six pieces of gold on top. When the six pieces are collected, the ninja will bounce away, speed up, or sometimes explode. You can also put down twelve pieces to allow the door to be used twice, eighteen would be three times, etc.


Thwumps

.....Just go between two thwumps, and you will go flying away. Occasionally you will get crushed though, but if you are half a tile in the thwumps, it should work. Another type, which is incredibly useful, is if you hit the corner, you will fly off, much like corner kicking. Very helpful when you want to change direction without a launchpad.


Locked door

.....More complicated. Also very unpredictable, but useful sometimes.

1. Make a locked door
2. Go to the debug menu and have a look at the level data, the last part should look something like this: 9^210,588,0,0,7,21,1,0,-1
3. Ignore most of it, you only need the third number after the ^. (it should be a 0 or a 1.) Change that number to read NaN. (not a number). The code should now look something like this: 9^210,588,NaN,0,7,21,1,0,-1
4. Press 'L' to load the edited level data.
5. Try it out.


Other

.....There are other types of propulsion, for example glitched tile propulsion, but Jiggerjaw12358 probably doesn't want me to give away how they are done.

FOR A MORE EXTENSIVE PROPULSION GUIDE, CLICK HERE

Tips
  • ALWAYS place your enemies first. (except propulsion enemies eg. thwumps.) If you try to add enemies later, it may not only not work, but screw up earlier enemies as well.
  • Press 1 before you play. This prevents enemies like chaingun drones from screwing up.
  • Don't have long waits. No one likes those.
  • Don't use gold delay. I don't mind it actually, but it can lower your rating quite a bit.
  • Try to make long DDAs. (at least 1000 frames)
  • Don't EVER include teleporters. They are a cheap way to get out of a tough place.
  • A very common mistake: Make sure you reset your level data.
  • Use a wide range of propulsions.
  • Make sure you use as much of the screen as possible.
  • Cheating death is a pain, unless you are actually basing the level on it, like this.
  • Oh and of course, practice. You rarely get a masterpiece on your first try.
  • Change your boss mode key. Hitting tab+q right at the end...
  • SAVE FREQUENTLY.
I want to hear all feedback/suggestions.


NOTE: Ignore the invisible .....'s at the start of every paragraph, I couldn't figure out another way to make indents.

Paste Name: What you want to call the level
Member Alias: Your nickname (TigerSlash-X)
Type: What type of level it is
Data: The map data
Demo: A demo (optional)
Comment: Any comments you might have
Password:* A password (I reccomend you don't use this, it'll just confuse people)

#310000 8 2007-04-04T12:41:36Z Clifty
READ THE FAQs.
Quote: Keron Cyst, 2005-04-06T16:47:58Z
Q: "Member Titles?" What's a Member Title? huh.gif
A: You see how you started off with "Newbie," then progressed to "Member" with Post Count, and so on and so forth? That's the forum's default Member Title system. I can manually change it to stick to something else permanently. Check out how pariah's letting off the happiness, Tsukatu's a Retired B&T Tyrant, etc.

Q: Cool. How can I get one?
A: We don't just hand Member Titles out like free passes to an amusement park (I used to, but that was when I was a moron, back in the day biggrin.gif). One of these two must occur, two of them unfortunately on your part:

  1. You must have done something very drastic in the community that affected numerous members (this can be a good or bad thing), like piss off a lot of people without actually breaking any forum rules, win a NTTS round or some other major contest, etc.
  2. You must be ber-1337 in some field, like working wonders with Paint, being a math genius, or helping Keron Cyst with his homework in the IRC room! biggrin.gif
  3. (this counts as a third but it's so rare that I didn't label it above) An admin can give one at random to a member, whether he wants it or not. Heheh, that's always fun.

But at any given time you can lose your title or it can be replaced with something else, so don't take it for granted!

Obviously skip me, because I don't have one.

#316407 2 2007-04-28T06:38:31Z Clifty
This is a common bug. The most likely cause are NaN's (glitches) in the level code. To fix, just go through the level code and replace all instances of 'NaN' with a number, eg. 276. Then, just delete all the items that appear in new spots, and replace them where they should go.

That's a complicated description, hopefully you'll be able to understand.

#318245 2 2007-05-04T22:30:00Z Clifty
Well, first off this should be in N help, not in Developers' Tutorials.
As for the question, to play it in debug mode, go to the debug menu, press R if your objects aren't in the right places, press 1, and then press P. Make sure you have capslock on, or it will be stuck in frame by frame mode.
To exit playing mode, just press ~.

For any more questions, just consult the manual.

#375966 2 2007-11-01T07:55:42Z Clifty
First off, hi, and welcome to the forums! Be sure to check out the forum rules.

In answer to your question, bonzai provided a nice route for this map here. You can find the majority of routes either in the pinned threads, or at the N wikia.

Also, just for next time, the regular format for levels is actually [episode]-[level], not [episode]:[level]. So, 92-3, not 92:3.

Hope this helps.

EDIT: It's not a live chat thing, by the way. Not everyone'll be looking at your thread at once. Sometimes, you'll have to wait hours for a response.

#413045 16 2008-03-01T11:49:26Z Clifty
*bump*

Well, 16 days, thousands of game crashes, and one save file wipe later, I've become The Guy!
Great, great game. Despite being incredibly evil, it was a really fun game to play. I reckon the best aspect of it was the sheer randomness. There was something new and different on EVERY single screen of the game, whether it was Ryu, or a plane that appeared from nowhere to blast you to smithereens.
This game must have been pretty hard to make, actually. It had to be evil, but not so unbelievibly hard that it would require luck, or frame by frame perfection. It had to make you keep going. And there couldn't be massive difficulty spikes (the one part where I think this did happen was in the G&G section, with the moving spikes. That was just insanely difficult.)

Oh, and:









Brilliant.
#413238 20 2008-03-02T03:30:52Z Clifty
Quote: Ad, March 01, 2008 10:58 pm
I started playing this again yesterday. I've beat BoweserWillyWart combo in one save, Tyson, and ContraBirdo in other. I'm at Dracula now. I've got two of those item things too. What're they for?

Oh, the secret items. They don't actually have any purpose, but they're just fun to collect. Which ones do you have?

Here's some spoilers!
Spoiler: all of the bosses, and how to beat them
(listed in chronological order. You can play most of the bosses in any order, but I'll also give the path that most people take, it's probably the easiest.)

1. Mike Tyson. He's the first boss of the upper path, directly after the room with the crazy plane.
Ok, well, he'll try to uppercut you. In his first stage, you're a lot faster than him, so just run to the left, dodging his uppercuts. When he breaths fire at you (I reckon he looks like he's crying actually. ) shoot him five times in the head, he'll go down. Stay where you are.
Second round- He'll move a little faster. Stay where you are for the first uppercut, then jump over, and run to the right. He'll try to hit you, but I think if you stay on the ground it doesn't kill you, as long as you're a little bit in front of him. Once you're on the right hand side, just shoot at him when he cries again. Easy.
Third Round - Ooooooh... Well, this one's a little hard. Try staying on the right for two uppercuts, and then run over to the left for the third, but sometimes this doesn't work. Be careful, he's faster than you on this one. Just practice, and work out a strategy, I have one, but it's really hard to explain and sometimes doesn't work.
Once you've finished, go to the right, get the orb, and then go back to the left to save. If you don't get the orb first, you'll have to play him again.

2. Mecha Birdo - Directly after the G&G area.
Right, well she's got three stages as well. (I think it's a she.) First, you just aim like crazy for that antenna. Not too hard, just make sure the stupid shy guys don't knock you off your platforms.
Stage 2 - Missiles move faster, and laser eyes as well! This time, aim like crazy for the eyes. About thirty shots should do it. Sorry, not really that much advice I can give, except make sure you get the most out of your jumps, or you won't make the gap between the missiles.
Stage 3 - Oh no, active missiles! Don't touch the fronts, or you'll die. Also, more shy guys, and I think the missiles move faster too. Well, just aim like crazy for the mouth, not really any more advice for ya though. After everything stops, just wait, and you'll go inside mecha birdo automatically, or.. something.

3. Dracula - Ugh. After the Castlevania area.
Ok, it's a two stage fight, but that doesn't make it any easier. It takes a bit of practice, but it becomes a lot easier if you learn all his moves, and how to dodge them. Stupid giant white balls. (ew...)
Well, first stage is fairly easy, but then the brains come along. Well, to avoid them, I try to get the both on one side of me, and then make them merge by shooting them together. Makes them a lot easier to manage.
No advice here, sorry.
Also, the kid's voice is the greatest thing ever. THE GREATEST THING EVERRRRRRRR

4. Hmm.. Kraidgeif would be next, I suppose. Go through the tetris room, and the kirby area.
He's a mix between Zangeif, and something with Kraid in it's name, not sure what.
Three stage battle. Not difficult, once you get the hang of it. Stage one - Aim for the face. In order to dodge his attacks, go down to the bottom left. Aim for the face, again. Dodge. Aim for the- yeah.
Stage TWO - He has a variety of attacks. Aim for the eyes, and he'll step backwards. When he attacks dodge (obviously). Then, he'll crouch, aim for the face again. When he runs forward, he'll smash the blocks, that's the start of...
Stage THREE - Blankas. From the crotch. I'm not joking. Jump on top of them, and shoot the face like crazy. LIKE CRAZY. He should blow up, and you can run across the floor to the right hand side.

5. The next two are pretty interchangable, but let's say you do the Klown Kar first. You can either take the bottom path from the start, or just keep going after Kraidgeif. Three stage fight, AGAIN.
STAGE 1 - Bowser - Easy. Stay on the right hand side, and then, when the bomb appears, shoot it into him. Very easy.
STAGE 2 - Wart - Easy. Dodge the shockwaves, and then shoot like hell when that thing appears.
STAGE 3 - Wily - HARD. Dodge the flames, then, when the giant bowling ball thing appears, stay on the left. Shooting it make the bullets reflect upwards and hit the boss. Then jump over the ball when it's about to crush you (I find this easiest to do when you're on the left hand side, but if you're on the right, then you'll shoot the ball quicker. Up to you, I guess.
VERY IMPORTANT::::::: Grab the orb, and walk back to the left hand side. It seems like you can't save, but you can: Jump upwards on the, and you'll go back to the save point. I beat the boss, and didn't save... had to do him again.

6. Mother Brain. Ok, I'll give you a quick guide on how to get there, this one took me a while to find. When you're in the factory path (with the DUMB BUGZ), and you have to ride a platform up in a column of spikes? (right before the yellow laser room). Well, if you jump down the hole below the platform, after you've ridden it up, then you can do a few more rooms, and end up down at Tourain. Stupid room.
Well, no advice here really. It's only a single stage, but it's damn, damn hard. You have to avoid the circle things and the guns, and you have to shoot the brain too. And there's hundreds of them. The only good thing is that M.B. doesn't take many hits. Well, good luck, I guess.

7. Dragon Devil. This one has to be second last, because you can't get to it until you've beaten the other six. In the palace of the guy, right after the mine carts. Well, he's not too hard. Possibly one of the easier bosses, actually.
Shoot whenever he has his mouth open. Dodge his fireballs. When he turns into the six fireball things (OH GOD AWFUL GRAPHICS), you have to work out the best route to dodge them, it's pretty hard to get a route that works consistantly. He'll do this three times, and then chase you back to the start, where he'll crash and die if you've shot him enough times. WHATEVER YOU DO DON'T MOVE AFTER THIS, OR YOUR GAME WILL GLITCH. You'll be teleported to the Entrance of the guy. Good luck.....

8. The Guy. At the very end. A massive SIX stage battle.
First, he'll shoot a large bullet at you. Dodge it, and drop down to the ground. You can dodge him while he tramples you, but I have a better way: If you stay close to him, right near the pillar on the left hand side, and shoot like crazy, you don't have to dodge him, he'll just go right onto stage two!
STAGE 2 - Ok, now you have to jump up to the ledge on the left. Wall climb, and stay up the top to dodge his stupid bullets. Once he jumps over to the left, jump down to the bottom. Run over to the right, but stay on the ground. When he shoots his GIANT bullets at you, jump over them, and shoot at the same time. If you hit him enough he'll go onto stage three (you'll know, he'll jump down onto the bottom), but more likely, you'll end up having to repeat stage 2, just do the same thing.
STAGE 3 - He should be down on the ground, in the middle. He'll shoot large, but not giant bullets at you. Now, the trick is that these bullets reflect towards you when they hit a wall, so the way to do it is to jump over them, and make them reflect towards him! Do this four times, and you'll kill him.
Victory! But...
STAGE 4 - OH NO HE'S LIKE 50 TIMES BIGGER. Aim for the eyes. Dodge the fire. Not too hard, really.
STAGE 5 - Well, this'll start with him shooting fire jets out of his eyes. They always travel anti-clockwise, so if you can get on the other side, then you're safe. Then, after he's stopped, shoot the eyes, and the white things.
STAGE 6 - Very very hard. His eyebrows block the path between the eyes, so you have to go around them, above the screen, dodging spikes, fire and white thingoes all the time. Shoot both the eyes to win.
And yes, he does say "I had sex with your mother". O_O

The final apple. Yes, I was dumb enough not to dodge this, so I had to do it again. Stupid ending credits.
Spoiler: all of the secrets, and how to get them
(listed from easiest to hardest, in my opinion.)

1. In the game over room. If you head to the left two rooms after the start on the upper path, (instead of riding the spikey platform up and dodging the spikes in the narrow column) you can jump over the pit of spikes by touching invisible platforms. (search) It's probably the easiest one to get.

2. Right after the hall of former guys. (directly after the fake 'game crash', that made me laugh. ) Just before the incinerator, and final tower of the guy. There's a save point you can't get to, so you think it's just there to trick you. But, if you fall directly down on the left hand side, and then jump off the screen to the left below the bridge, there's a secret platform, which will take you to the save point. After that there's a room, with the secret item at the end. This one's meant to be the hardest to get, I reckon it's a lot easier than number 6.

3. In the tourain area. In the first room of that area (right after the invisible block room), wait for a while, and weird thing will come out of the wall. Go in through where it came out, and you'll be in the room. I'll leave you to figure this one out, but it's quite hard to get right.

4. In the G&G area (with the zombies and tombstones). You know the hole where you fall down to start playing Mecha birdo? Well, instead of jumping down there, you can jump up the right hand side of the screen. It's a hard jump to make, but the room you jump into is even harder. The most spikes I've ever seen. ;_;

5. In the kirby area. After getting into the cave, don't save. You only get one chance to do this. The truck will keep driving, and smash a hole in the right hand side of the screen, If you follow it, you can get into the secret cavern. But, if you die on the way, the hole will seal up, so don't save there, save before the truck. (you can also get this item through a glitch in the tetris room, but I haven't pulled that off...)

6. Right after the tourain area. I can't get this one, although I can get to the room where it is. After the 'snake' room, you get to the room with the couches that launch you upwards, right before the palace of the guy. Well, if you hold jump while on those couches, they launch you further, and take you all the way up to the secret room.

Anything else anyone needs any help with, just ask me.

#20961 1 2008-07-31T07:26:18Z Clifty
I'm working on my first flash game, and it's gone reasonably well so far. Physics are all working, however, for some reason, and I can't figure out why, shooting is not.
I got the code I use for shooting directly from a tutorial somewhere on the internet, so I'm not really sure how it works. It's all pretty basic stuff, but I'm pretty new to this, so I don't really get some of it. I'm fairly sure with most of it, not so much with the duplicateMovieClip() stuff.

Anyway, here's the code on the bullet (instance name 'shota'):

onClipEvent(load)
{
	if(this._name=="shota")
	{
  this._visible=false;
	}
	else
	{
  this._visible=true;
	}
	this._x=_root.cursor_mc._x;
	this._y=_root.cursor_mc._y;
	shotaxspeed=20*Math.sin(_root.cursor_mc._rotation*(Math.PI/180));
	shotayspeed=20*Math.cos(_root.cursor_mc._rotation*(Math.PI/180));
}
onClipEvent(enterFrame)
{
	this._x+=shotaxspeed;
	this._y-=shotayspeed;
	if(this._x<-200)
	{
  this.removeMovieClip();
	}
	if(this._x>1000)
	{
  this.removeMovieClip();
	}
	if(this._y>800)
	{
  this.removeMovieClip();
	}
	if(this._y<-200)
	{
  this.removeMovieClip();
	}
	
	
	

}


And here's the code on the enemy "enemya" :

onClipEvent (enterFrame) {
	if (_root.shota.hitTest(this)) {
    	_root.score += 1;
  _root.enemyahp -= 1;
  }
  

}

And lastly, the code on the player, 'cursor_mc'

onClipEvent(mouseDown) {
	mousedown += 1;
    if (_root.score <= 499) {
  _root.shota.duplicateMovieClip( "shota"+shotacount, shotacount+7000);
    	_root["shota"+shotacount]._visible=true;
    	shotacount++;
    	if(shotacount>100) {
    shotacount=1;
    	}
	}
}

Anyway, when the bullets hit the enemy, they just go through it, and don't deal damage.
It's probably a dumb mistake, but like I said before, I'm not quite sure how the duplicateMovieClip stuff works.

#456615 3 2008-07-31T12:01:13Z Clifty
Quote: mattk210, July 31, 2008 08:09 pm
well, mc.duplicatemovieclip("myName",depth) is the format, it copies mc into a new movieclip with name "myName".
the name is shota+a number when duplicating
so testing for a hit with shota won't return a collision with that mc.

Ah yeah, thanks. I had a feeling that was where the problem was.

Quote: mattk210, 2008-07-31T10:09:39Z
To be honest, that's some pretty messy code.

Yeah, I knew it probably would be. This is basically the first time I've used actionscript beyond gotoAndPlay, so a lot is just block pasted from various tutorials. ;_;
Quote: mattk210, 2008-07-31T10:09:39Z
I would suggest a complete rewrite but barring that, an array of shots should work -- have a shot movieclip in your library, attach it to the stage when a shot is fired and store it in a variable in the array. Then for hit detection loop through the array.

setting up the array:
var shotArray:Array=new Array()

assigning a movieclip to an element of the array (replace i with a number)
shotArray=_root.attachMovie(ID, name, depth)

You may not have understood that, try reading up on it or i can walk you through the correct way to program this game.

Yeah, I didn't understand that. ;_; I know I'm kinda saying the same thing over and over, but I really don't know what I'm doing half the time. Redoing it might be wise, I dunno, you probably have a much better idea than me.

But really, you should look up some good programming principles and stuff. Movieclips and rendering stuff on screen should be discrete from the logic of your game.
